### Page 1

Nanya Technology

Upbeat 3Q margin/ASP, and 2027 guidance 
also optimistic: lift PO to NT$800

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 800.00 TWD | Price: 527.00 TWD

3Q results confirm super-cycle even with legacy DRAM 
Nanya Tech, a pure DRAM supplier, reported very strong 3Q results (sales: NT$134bn, 
+62% QoQ / +612% YoY; OPM: 80% - similar to big-3 DRAM makers’; net margin: 66%). 
The key contributor should be upbeat ASP (up 60%+ QoQ vs consensus of 30-40%). In 
fact, Nanya Tech had already reported more than doubled 1H ASP (60%+ QoQ for two 
consecutive quarters). Thus, Street expected limited QoQ price increase in 3Q (vs 1H).  
According to Nanya Tech President Pei Ing Lee, demand for legacy DRAM products 
(DDR3 & DDR4) remained strong across IT, industrial, and enterprise applications. Mgmt 
also said legacy DRAM accounted for 80%+ of 3Q sales (vs 10% range for DDR5). Net-
net, the 3Q results confirmed super-cycle even selling mainly legacy/conventional DRAM. 
Guidance broadly bullish; no sign of a price cut 
Lee provided bullish guidance (earnings call). What we learned: (1) rising 4Q ASP, (2) 
ongoing shortage of legacy DRAM through 2027 (no immediate demand shift from 
DDR4 to DDR5), (3) new fab mass production (2027-28), (4) significant FCF generation 
(with high margin profile) despite large capex spend (NT$70bn in 2026), (5) record-high 
net cash (NT$270bn in 3Q), (6) new node development (in-house) and migration (1c, 1d, 
1e) on track, and (7) new construction plan for 3D wafer packaging/testing facilities in 
Ping Tung Science Park, Taiwan. Overall, new guidance presents further growth (capacity 
expansion) and high margin profile (favorable ASP trend). 
Lift PO to NT$800 on revisions to 2027/28 EPS estimates 
We lift our PO from NT$750 to NT$800 using the same target P/E vs earlier (9x 2027-
28E EPS). Thus, our PO revision is mostly based on 7-8% revisions to 2027-28 EPS 
estimates. We assume 5%/1ppt higher sales/OPM vs our earlier forecasts given more 
favorable conventional DRAM ASP trend. To be conservative, we assume negative EPS 
growth for 2028 but it can turn positive if the ASP remains stable. We reiterate Buy.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Nanya Technology is an affiliate of Formosa Plastics Group. 
The firm manufactures and distributes DRAM chips. 
Management focuses on non-PC applications such as 
consumer, mobile, server, autos, etc. Key customers are 
based in China and Taiwan. Samsung, SK Hynix and Micron 
are major competitors.

Investment Rationale 
Our Buy rating is based on the DRAM industry's supercycle, 
which should warrant a high-margin profile for Nanya Tech 
even in the long term. Its 3Q26 results proved record-high 
revenue (NT$134bn, more than 5x higher than the previous 
peak cycle) and 80% operating margin. Management focus 
on long-term contracts, including DDR4 for eSSD or servers, 
should also act as a catalyst. Significantly rising net cash 
even after capex hike could also support higher dividend 
payouts.

Stock Data  
Price to Book Value 3.7x

Exhibit 25: The BofA Memory Indicator still presents super-cycle – much higher than 2017-18 
peak level; of course, it slightly declined to 179 in Aug-26 (due to lower YoY growth) after 
hitting peak of 190 in Mar/Apr-26, driven by exceptionally strong DRAM/NAND spot pricing, 
rising ASPs and billings, along with Korea’s ~200%+ export growth. 
BofA Memory Indicator – back to upturn level since Oct-2025 and hit highest peak in Mar/Apr-26 
(back-tested)

Note: Our indicator had previously been capped at ~140, but the recent unprecedented surge—driven by memory spot prices, ASPs, and 
billings—has led us to raise the ceiling (up to 240 levels) to reflect a more accurate relative comparison. This exceptional strength is 
further validated by strong earnings from memory chipmakers.

### Page 16

Price objective basis & risk 
Nanya Technology (NNYAF) 
Our PO of NT$800 is derived from 9x 2027-28E P/E, which is c.30% below the long-
term historical average (13x during 2014-25), but near-50% above the previous upturn 
period (6x in 2014-18). Memory stocks usually trade at low valuation multiples during 
peak cycle due to downturn concerns. However, the current DRAM cycle looks 
structurally much better than ever and we expect 2027-28 earnings to exceed 2026 level 
due to the AI growth theme. This could lead to a re-rating (e.g., 9x P/E or higher), with 
solid earnings outlook.

Upside risks: DRAM price increases even after the 1H26 upturn, Big-3 memory 
chipmakers' significant supply cut for commodity DRAM due to HBM, and Nanya Tech's 
more successful ramp-up of DDR4 or DDR5 (better cost or margin improvement).

Downside risks: weaker legacy DDR4 demand (quicker shift to DDR5), and China's more 
successful memory production increase.

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings ar e: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation). 
Investment rating Total return expectation (within 12-month period of date of initial 
rating)

Neutral ≥ 0% ≤ 30%

INCOME RATINGS, indicators of potential cash dividends, are: 7 - same/higher (dividend considered to be secure), 8 - same/lower (dividend not considered to be 
secure) and 9 - pays no cash dividend.  Coverage Cluster is comprised of stocks covered by a single analyst or two or more analysts sharing a common industry, 
sector, region or other classification(s). A stock’ s coverage cluster is included in the most recent BofA Global Research report referencing the stock.

Industry View In-Line
We keep our 3Q26 iPhone build estimate unchanged at 54M units (+4% q/q, -2%
y/y). But we have made adjustments to new iPhone 18 builds by lowering iPhone
Duo by 0.5M units to 0.5M, while raising iPhone 18 Pro Max by 0.5M units to 8.5M.

For 4Q, we lower our iPhone build estimate by 1M units to 72M units (+33% q/q,
-5% y/y), reflecting lower legacy iPhone models (iPhone 16/17e). Total new iPhone
18 model family units remains at 71M units (-21% y/y) in 2H26, with one less SKU
launched compared to last year. We continue to expect 7M units of iPhone Duo
builds in 2H26. With iPhone Duo sales starting soon, we will continue to monitor
ramp-up progress for any upside or downside to builds. Our revised full-year iPhone

build forecast implies CY26 shipment at 234M units (+3% y/y).
Our 3Q26 iPad build forecast is lowered by 0.5M units to 11.5M units (-12% q/q, -
18% y/y). We lowered 3Q entry level iPad builds due to  pull-forward demand in 1H
and the impact of rising prices. We also lower our 4Q iPad build forecast by 0.7M
units to 10.3M units (-10% q/q, -26% y/y). Our revised full-year iPad build forecast
implies CY26 shipments at 46.8M units (-14% y/y).

3Q26e iPhone build unchanged at 54M units (+4% q/q, -2% y/y).
4Q26e iPhone build lowered slightly to 72M units (+33% q/q, -5% y/y).
3Q26e iPad build lowered slightly to 11.5M units (-12% q/q, -18% y/y).
4Q26e iPad build lowered to 10.3M units (-10% q/q, -26% y/y).

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

### Page 3

TSMC (2330.TW)
15 October 2026 Citi Research
3
Rather than now slowing down, it’s actually getting stronger
TSMC reported an upbeat 3Q26 result with robust 4Q26 outlook. It guided for
4Q26 revenue of US$52.3–54.3 billion, representing about 14% QoQ growth and
58% year-over-year growth at the midpoint (based on FX at NT$31.8 per USD).
4Q26 GM/OPM is expected at 65–67% and 56.5–58.5%. TSMC reiterated its 2026
capex to be US$60–64 billion and said investment from 2026 to 2028 will be

significantly higher than in the previous three years, reflecting strong multi-year
customer demand, including agentic AI. It is also worth noting that TSMC plans to
begin using the High-NA EUV technology around 2030 and develop a 12-inch
mask capability by 2031, when it expects the tools to offer sufficient maturity and
cost effectiveness. Management said this timing does not undermine its
confidence in technology leadership.
Management described AI-related demand as exceptionally strong, supported by

accelerators, networking chips, and rising demand for CPUs associated with
agentic AI. TSMC’s previously guided AI GPU/accelerator CAGR to be mid-to-high
50%, but now sees more upside ahead. Given rising concern on AI expanding
peaking out, the management said it is clear that its capacity planning strategy has
considered customer forecasts, data-center construction, power availability, and
other supply-chain constraints. Management still judges demand to be above

available supply and is working to close the gap, while emphasizing disciplined
planning to limit the risk of overbuilding.
Figure 1. TSMC – 3Q26 results comparison
© 2026 Citigroup Inc. No redistribution without Citigroup’s written permission.
Source: Citi Research, Bloomberg
Capacity expansion plan and allocation
During the earnings call, TSMC also mentioned that despite the tight capacity
supply, customers cannot generally sublet or auction wafer capacity, while there is

limited case that collaboration is through mask-license agreements. For the
advanced packaging, TSMC has announced its Global Foundries and VIS’
agreement on interposer as part of its backend supply strategy, intended to help
ensure customers have enough capacity to bring products to market; It also
reiterated that TSMC’s CoWoS remains as the leading customer’s choice and is
already in volume production at 5x reticle size, while it is developing 9x and larger

solutions and evaluating CoPoS.

### Page 4

TSMC (2330.TW)
15 October 2026 Citi Research
4
Reiterate Buy, best position in AI arena
Our earnings projection is largely unchanged for 2027 /2028 earnings. While its
capex expansion, overseas operation and N2 will likely be a margin dilution factor
initially, due to its cross-node leverage, high UTR and yield rate, we believe its GM
downside is manageable. Overall, we see strong advanced node demand into next
year and the long-term order visibility will be supported for higher capex cycle.  We

maintain our TP to NT$4,000, based on unchanged 25x of 2027 EPS estimate;
reiterate Buy.
Key TSMC earnings call recap
n4Q26 guidance
–Sales: US$52.3-54.3bn (+14% QoQ at the midpoint)
–GPM: 65-67% (assuming USDTWD=31.8)
–OPM: 56.5-58.5%
n2026 full-year & long-term guidance
–Full year sales: raise 2026 full-year USD-based sales guidance from more
than 40% growth to +44% growth
–Full year capex: retain its 2026 capex guidance at US$60-64bn (70-80%
advanced process, 10% specialty, 10-20% for advanced packaging, testing

and masks)
–GM outlook
•4Q GM to be impacted by N2 and overseas fab ramps, but partially
supported by productivity improvement, UTR enhancement and
optimizing capacity allocation
•GM dilution from overseas fab would be c2ppts impact for the full-year
2026; with more new capacity expansion in the US, the margin dilution
impact would widen to 3-4ppts in the next few years.
–Long-term outlook
•Retains 5-year AI sales CAGR (2024-2029) remains at high-50% or above

nCapacity planning
–TSMC currently has 20 fabs in total. It plans to add 13 fabs in Taiwan and add
5-6 overseas fabs.
–N2 capacity will double in 2027; it has also started preparing for A14 capacity
with volume production starting from 2028.
–US Arizona fab 2 construction is complete and begin volume production in
3Q27, ahead of schedule.
–JP fab 2 would produce N3 with mass production from 2028

Catalyst: Thematic driven
TSMC will host 3Q26 earnings call on Oct 15. We expect TSMC to beat its recently raised guidance once again. Upbeat
guidance, robust demand for advanced nodes into next year, and a higher capex cycle should provide further upside
potential for the share price in the coming months.

### Page 10

TSMC (2330.TW)
15 October 2026 Citi Research
10
TSMC
Company description
TSMC is the founder and leader of the dedicated IC foundry segment. The
company has built its reputation by offering advanced and "more-than-
Moore" wafer production processes and industry-leading manufacturing
efficiency. From its inception in 1987, TSMC has consistently offered the
foundry segment's leading technologies and TSMC-compatible design
services.
Investment strategy

We rate TSMC shares as Buy. We expect TSMC to deliver sales growth of
>40% in recent years thanks to strong growth from HPC/AI as well as stable
demand growth from smartphone, Io T, and automotive on tech migration and
a shortened replacment cycle. CPU outsourcing to TSMC offers further
growth potential. A rising cash dividend should also support the share price.
While TSMC has been increasing its capex meaningfully since 2009, its cost
structure remaisn competitive through continuous product mix

improvements, efficiency enhancements, and cost reductions.
Valuation
Our target price for TSMC shares of NT$4,000 is based on 25x our 2027E
EPS, higher than its three-year average forward PER, versus global peers’
average of 16-25x. We believe our target PER is justified by TSMC's leading
position in advanced process node and robust AI demand growth outlook.
Given its continuing leadership in the semiconductor foundry industry, we
believe TSMC’s stable order visibility and earnings outlook make it less

vulnerable to a potential global economic downturn than some of its peers.
Our target price equates to 2026E/27E P/E of ~ 36x/25x and 2026E/27E P/B of
~13x/10x.
Risks
Key downside risks that could impede the shares from achieving our target
price include: 1) weakness in the global semiconductor market; 2) larger-
than-expected margin contractions due to depreciation cost increases and
strong NTD; 3) competitors entering the foundry business; 4) longer-than-

expected digestion of inventory in the supply chain; and 5) a slowdown in
demand either due to a global economic downturn or a global trade
disruption triggered by tariffs.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

### Page 1

MediaTek

ASIC business expanding into Bytedance; 
potential high-end mobile gains in ‘27

Maintain Rating: BUY | PO: 6,200 TWD | Price: 4,475 TWD

Bytedance expanding ASIC TAM beyond Google/xAI 
Bloomberg reported on October 15th that Bytedance is close to an agreement to work 
with Mediatek on the ASIC design and turnkey service support following its collaboration 
with Broadcom and Qualcomm. This is consistent with our update in September that 
Mediatek could co-develop an AI inference chipset with Bytedance from 1H27 for tape 
out in 1H28 and see contribution from production in early ’29. Similar to Mediatek and 
xAI’s collaboration, we believe Bytedance would need the support on sourcing 
components (e.g. HBM and substrate) in addition to wafer manufacturing/packaging, 
lifting the dollar content vs. Google’s TPUs. Assuming 1mn unit demand annually & 
US$20-25k ASPs including advanced logic dies and HBM, the project could contribute 
US$20-25bn to Mediatek’s sales in ’29 (vs. BofA sales expectation on Mediatek  of 
US$82bn in ’28 with US$45bn from Google TPUv8/v9 and US$9bn from xAI’s 1-2 
quarter contribution). 
A modest tailwind in mobile as it gains share at Samsung 
The mobile chipset builds from Mediatek and Qualcomm in 2H26 are tracking below 
seasonal in 2H26 as the end demand is impacted by the elevated memory cost. While 
the market outlook looms into ’27 (vs. -25% Android unit demand in ’26), we expect 
Mediatek’s mobile business to fare better especially in the high-end segment where it 
could gain unit share by penetrating into Samsung’s flagship smartphones historically 
dominated by Qualcomm and Samsung’s own SoCs. Factoring 30% share in Samsung’s 
high-end mobile shipment of 30-35mn annual units and US$200+ chipset ASPs, it could 
be ~20% accretive to Mediatek’s mobile sales and ~5% of its total business from 2H27. 
EPS and valuation downside limited after recent pullback 
We maintain our Buy rating & NT$6,200 PO on 24x 2H27-1H28E P/E (implied 30x ASIC 
EPS + 10x merchant EPS). The share has been dragged by TPU competition concern in 
the near term but could be lifted by expanding ASIC projects and high-end mobile gains.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
MediaTek is a fabless IC design company supplying to 
wireless communications and digital consumer markets. 
Before taking leadership in mobile SoC area, it had built 
solid ground in the TV SoC and optical disk drive markets. 
The firm invests heavily in R&D to seek better positioning 
in the 5G cycle, and has a complete offering including ASIC, 
Wi-Fi, PMIC etc. to address applications beyond 
smartphone, such as IoT and auto.

Investment Rationale 
We have Buy rating on MediaTek as we think MediaTek is 
ready and has the strength to brace for the upcoming 
proliferation of IoT devices and opportunities in growing 
markets like ASIC, ARM-based PC, auto, and etc., and we 
believe its solid balance sheet and cashflow could provide 
downside support to the share price. Meanwhile, we expect 
5G smartphone penetration to be a multi-year theme for 
the company, with its better competitive landscape in 
duopoly 5G era and an improved margin profile.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
MediaTek (MDTKF) 
Our PO of NT$6,200 is based on 24x 2H27-1H28 P/E. which is towards the higher end 
of its historical P/E range of 8-25x, as we believe the company's broadening ASIC 
pipeline could drive an earnings upgrade cycle.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) better than expected smartphone demand, especially in 
China, (2) a better pricing in mobile communication segment thanks to alleviated 
competition and rising exposure to high-end/flagship market, and (3) an even more 
superior investment return on non-op side to uplift its net cash position. (4) Unexpected 
collaboration with global technology leaders leads to higher than expected 
margins/profits

Downside risks to our PO are: (1) weaker-than-expected end-market situation in 
consumer-related smartphone/TV space, (2) macro challenges including inflation/policy-
related pressures, (3) rising competition from existing players and/or newcomers in 
smartphone/TV SoC (system on chip) space, and (4) ASP/margin pressure caused by each 
or all of the above and/or rising foundry costs. (4) Bidding process for Meta Inference 
ASIC is slower than expected.

potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment rating s are: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things,

壹、投 資 建 議 
重點摘要： 
 統新 2Q26 營收為 2.08 億元，+34.7%QoQ、+82.4%YoY，毛利率
躍升至 56.20%，稅後淨利 0.86 億元，單季稅後 EPS+2.22 元。
2Q26 受惠生產效率提升、產能滿載毛利率躍升。 
 AI 伺服器帶動統新的 DWDM 產品延伸至 AI 資料中心 DCI 主要
骨幹濾片，光纖波段分離與光纖傳輸頻寬增加，會需要統新薄
膜濾光片作為訊號傳輸強化。除 Scale-Across 長距離骨幹，分
散式 AI 數據中心，專門做長距離光通訊傳輸 DWDM 市場，
2026~2031 年每年維持 31%年複合成長。 
 1.6T 光纖收發模組 ， 未來 2 年呈倍數增長 。 1.6T 光纖收發模組，
在後端 PD(接收端)改採 200G PD，會用到 8 片統新之薄膜濾光
片。

一. 統新 2Q26 營收與財報優於市場預期，3Q26 續強 
統新 2Q26 營收為 2.08 億元，+34.7%QoQ、+82.4%YoY，毛利率躍
升至 56.20%，稅後淨利 0.86 億元，單季稅後 EPS+2.22 元。主要受
惠於 AI 資料中心互連需求，使得統新產線維持滿載。展望 3Q26
預估統新營收為 2.42 億元，+16.35%QoQ，毛利率 56.58%，稅後淨
利 1.06 億元，單季稅後 EPS+2.76 元。統新預計產能增加 30%，以
因應客戶需求。

二. 2026 年 DWDM 隨 AI 骨幹網絡建置，濾片量增價漲 
統新高密度波長多工器( D W D M，D e n s e  W a v e l e n g t h  D i v i s i o n  
Multiplexing)，至少可傳輸 16 種不同顏色的光纖，最高可傳輸 128
種不同顏色之光纖，同一條主幹網路上，可以傳輸的頻寬因此加
大，相當具有傳輸效率。DWDM 於發送端將不同波長的光信號組
合起來(覆用, MUX)，並耦合到光纜線路上的同一根光纖中，進行
傳輸，在接收端又將多個波長的光信號分開(解覆用, DEMUX)，並
作進一步處理，恢復出原波長信號後送入不同的終端接收器。統
新的 DWDM 產品延伸至 AI 資料中心 DCI 主要骨幹濾片，波段分
離 ( S -B a n d、C - B a n d 傳 輸 核 心、L -B a nd 容 量 擴 充、S -B a nd 未 來 預 留、
U-Band 8nm 用於 OTDR 光纖監控偵測主幹網路是否具有斷點或需
要救援)，統新以單通道 100GHz 去細分 DWDM 濾光片，統新推出
新品，50GHz 間隔濾光片，支援的通道數量提升至 96Channels。 
投資評等：買進 
目標價位：375.00 元

三.  AI 伺服器高速傳輸，帶動光通訊主被動元件量增 
Scale-Across 長距離骨幹，分散式 AI 數據中心，專門做長距離光
通訊傳輸 DWDM 市場，2026~2031 年，DWDM 市場每年維持 31%
年 複合 成長。此 外，受惠 AI 伺服 器傳 輸速 率提升，如：Broadcom、
Marvell 等國際晶片廠商，於 2026 年起已推出 800G /1.6T Switch 晶
片組，等同於 AI 伺服器後端 Ports 支援的光纖通訊傳輸會一併升
級至 800G 與 1.6T 傳輸速率。Yole 報告指出，1.6T 光纖收發模組
出貨量，2026 年/2027 年/2028 年預估量 15~20M/55~60M/85~100M，
1.6T 光纖收發模組 ， 未來 2 年內呈倍數增長 。 1.6T 光纖收發模組，
在後端 PD(接收端)改採 200G PD ， 會用到 8 片統新之薄膜濾光片。
在薄膜濾光片鍍膜層數 ， Scale-Across 與 NPO 所需薄膜濾光片層數
約 150L~200L，未來發展至 CPO 時鍍膜層數會再增加 8L，統新為
此預計多準備 10 條產線預計開發 250L+規格，予台系知名晶圓代
工廠商。統新指出，Scale-Out 濾片鍍膜代工量增加，是替中系系
統廠代工(層數約 100~150L)。Scale-Across 濾片鍍膜層數會大於
200L，甚至高達 250L，目前訂單能見度高，涵蓋 1.)同業產能難以
支應，Cohr 交給統新、2.)中系系統廠商加單、3.)台系網通廠商於
低軌衛星鍍膜(LEO 非光學鍍膜) 。統新毛利率之所以跳升，是先前
客戶給統新的試產量僅 20~30 片，2Q26 起量產量增至 K 級別，出
貨量增加、規模經濟效應之下，單月營收損平點(評估為 0.4~0.5
億元)，2Q26 起月營收顯著跳升至 0.6~0.7 億元。預期此營運動能
至少能見度到 4Q26，但由於 DWDM 交期延長至 6~8 個月，預估
2027 年產業供需偏向緊俏。

四.投資建議：買進，目標價 375 元 
統新競爭優勢在於其生產高品質高效率之薄膜濾光片篩選特定輻
射波段的光學元件，能篩選光的強度、改變光譜成分或限定震動
截面，藉由 CWDM/DWDM 技術使相同數據集中於同一根光纖中 
傳輸，增大傳輸容量進而提高光纖的使用效率，生產良率逐步提
升且具佔有一定之市場地位。預估統新 2026 年營收 8.56 億元，
+75.77%YoY，毛利率 53.43%，稅後淨利 3.32 億元，稅後 EPS 為
+8.63 元。展望 2027 年，隨產能積極擴充之下，預估統新年營收
為 17.02 億元，+98.83%YoY ，毛利率預估為 54.85% ，稅後淨利 5.57
億元，稅後 EPS+14.47 元。考量基礎建設帶動 DWDM 規格升級，
AI 伺服器傳輸速率提升因應 1.6T 傳輸薄膜濾光片用量同步增長，
給予統新投資建議買進，目標價 375 元(26.00 X2027 EPS)。

一.公司簡介

統新(6426)為生產精密光學元件，以及高密度分波多工器(DWDM)
關鍵元件─薄膜濾光片(TFF)之專業廠商。各產品線營收占比為主
動光學元件佔比低於 15%、被動光學元件：80~85%、光學鍍膜元
件與代工低於 10%。主要產品 DWDM 濾光片 (Dense Wavelength 
Division Multiplexer，即為高密度波長多工分工器)技術可以大幅提
高光纖通訊系統之頻寬，成為光纖通訊系統的市場中關注的焦
點。統新國內主要競爭對手為東典光電科技(股)，國外廠商包含
II-IV Cohernet(美國)、Iridian Technologies(加拿大)等廠商投入相關
產品研發及生產。

圖一、光通訊元件

資料來源：公司、宏遠投顧整理

二.26 年營運概況及 26 年展望 
1.統新 DWDM 技術層層堆疊良率優異  
統新主要產品為薄膜濾光片即為篩選特定輻射波段的光學元件，
能篩選光的強度、改變光譜成分或限定震動截面，藉由不同光線
原理而選擇通過的光源(紅、藍、綠光) ，且藉由 CWDM/DWDM 技
術使相同數據集中於同一根光纖中傳輸，增大傳輸容量進而提高
光纖的使用效率。

2.因應 AI 資料中心基建商機，薄膜濾光片產品走向更高規  
回顧 5G 時期的薄膜濾光片，支援頻段增加，切割更細，中心波

## Document 185: 9月经济数据跟踪：经济寻底接近尾声，政策托举四季度增速

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月经济数据跟踪：经济寻底接近尾声，政策托举四季度增速.pdf
Report source: 万联证券
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 13
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

[Table_RightTitle] 
宏观研究|宏观跟踪报告

[Table_Title] 
经济寻底接近尾声，政策托举四季度增速

[Table_ReportType] 
——9 月经济数据跟踪

[Table_ReportDate] 2026 年 10 月 19 日

[T able_Summary] ⚫ 事件：2026 年Q3,GDP 不变价同比4.5%，环比1.2%。工业增加值
同比由5.2%上行至5.5%，固投累计同比持平于-7.2%，其中制造
业、基建、房地产投资分别由-2.3%、-4%、-19.9%变动为-2.1%、
-4.2%、-20.6%，社零当月同比由0.4%上行至1.1%。 
⚫ 三季度GDP 略高于市场预期，全年经济目标有望达成。2026 年三
季度GDP 不变价同比增速为4.5%，高于市场预期的4.4%。第二、
三产业对GDP 同比拉动作用边际放缓，第一产业对GDP 同比拉动
作用加强。工业产能利用率环比上行，采矿业整体小幅回升，制
造业整体小幅回落，行业分化明显。 
⚫ 工业生产季节性回升，出口拉动作用走强，高技术产业生产高位
边际放缓。工业企业出口交货值大幅拉升，出口给部分行业生产
带来较强拉动。采矿业生产持续修复，石油开采业为主要拉动，
煤炭产量跌幅收窄。制造业生产整体小幅回落，上游制造业中，
非金属矿、黑色金属冶炼行业生产上行，极端天气影响消退，施
工旺季带来需求回补，政策性金融工具加速落地，带来行业边际
改善。化工在地缘政策不确定性影响下，企业补库需求提升，行
业生产上行，有色金属冶炼及压延工业、橡胶和塑料制品业在价
格走高等影响下，生产同比回落。中游制造业表现分化，整体仍
处高位，消费品制造业整体回落。下游制造业中，汽车、运输设
备、电子设备制造同比均回落，新质生产力生产边际放缓，新旧
动能分化收敛。 
⚫ 服务业生产指数回升，生产性服务表现仍强。“双节”给服务业
带来一定提振，服务业活动预期指数持平。商务活动指数上行，
信息传输、软件和信息技术服务业与租赁和商务服务业同比小幅
上行，租赁和运输业同比均有所修复。 
⚫ 投资止跌企稳，资金投放和落地稳步推进。随着稳增长政策逐步
落地，固定资产投资逐步止跌企稳。设备购置在政策推动下持续
上行，建安工程继续下跌，新开工偏弱，投资项目不足的影响仍
存。固投资金来源来看，今年以来债券累计同比维持高位，政府
专项债用于项目建设比重明显提升，预算内资金同比跌幅有所收
窄，项目资金逐步落地。 
⚫ 地产投资持续下滑，销售依然偏弱。地产新开工、竣工跌幅加
深，施工增速持平。地产销售“金九”行情表现偏弱，二手房销
售好于新房。一线城市和部分二线城市新房价格环比上行，三线
城市价格依然偏弱，二手房价格同比多数下跌，房价依然有待企
稳。9 月 29 日居民购房贷款贴息政策公布，有望给刚需带来一定
利好，政策效果仍有待观望。房企资金来源持续收紧，销售回款
和融资环境依然承压，房企拿地偏谨慎。四季度土地供应总量收
缩的特征预期持续，结构逐步改善。 
[Table_Authors] 分析师: 于天旭 
执业证书编号： S0270522110001

万联证券研究所 www.wlzq.cn 第 2 页 共 13 页  
⚫ 基建投资持续放缓，制造业投资反弹上行，财政资金有望给四季
度增速带来支撑。三季度以来财政资金投放有所提速，但落地进
程依然偏慢，基建下行斜率明显放缓。随着8000 亿元政策性金融
工具、政府专项债和地方政府债务结存限额在四季度投放并落
地，“六张网”建设进程有望提速，四季度基建将发挥“压舱
石”的作用。制造业投资上行部分对冲了地产、基建下行的压
力，高技术产业增速边际放缓。大规模设备更新政策、出口强韧
性，以及稳增长政策逐步落地给制造业带来拉动。 
⚫ 消费增速修复， “双节”带来提振，结构表现分化。服务消费好
转，餐饮增速上行，线上消费小幅放缓。居民实际可支配收入边
际上行，人均消费支出持平，居民消费信心窄幅震荡，内需整体
依然偏弱。可选消费结构分化，汽车为主要拖累，地产下游消费
偏弱。金银珠宝类本月跌幅收窄最为显著，通讯器材类消费持续
走高，假期出行需求提振下，石油及制品类跌幅收窄。必选消费
整体表现稳健，粮油、食品类、日用品类上行幅度较大。 
⚫ 四季度政策将发挥托底作用，关注政策落地效果。9 月我国经济
外需拉动、生产强、内需弱的格局持续，但新旧经济分化程度边
际收敛。9 月稳增长政策逐步加码，政府债发行提速，在政策扶
持下，四季度经济有望缓步回升，但仍需关注财政资金向实物工
作量的转化效率，预期全年经济节奏呈现“U 型”走势。 
⚫ 风险因素：地缘政治风险发展超预期，居民购房持续低迷，我国
政策出台和落地不及预期

事件 
2026年三季度，GDP不变价同比增速4.5%， 季调环比增长1.2%， 前值0.9%。9月，工业
增加值当月同比增速上行0.3个百分点至5.5%，环比上涨0.61%，前值为0.54%。前三
季度，固定资产投资累计同比增速-7.2%，8月为-7.2%，其中，房地产开发投资累计
同比增速回落0.7个百分点至-20.6%，基建投资累计同比增速回落 0.2个百分点至-
4.2%， 制造业投资增速累计同比上行0.2个百分点至-2.1%。 社会消费品零售总额同比
增速由上月的0.4%上行至1.1%，

1    三季度经济增速略高于市场预期 
三季度GDP增速略高于市场预期，外需强劲拉动生产为主要支撑，全年经济目标有望
达成。2026年三季度GDP不变价同比增速为4.5%，市场预期为4.4%，一、二季度同比
增速分别为5%、4.3%；三季度环比增速为1.2%， 高于前值0.9%， 前三季度累计同比为
4.6%。分产业看，第一、二、三产业累计同比增速分别由上半年的3.7%、3.9%、5.2%
变动为3.7%、3.8%、5.2%，仅第二产业同比增速小幅放缓。第二、三产业对GDP同比
拉动作用边际放缓，第一产业对GDP同比拉动作用加强。二季度以来，地缘政治扰动
给全球经济、 物价带来较大风险， 我国出口保持强劲，给工业生产带来拉动， 为经济
重要支撑。 原油等大宗商品涨价压力加大， 输入性通胀被动抬升，我国工业品价格上
涨，CPI总体表现温和。 
工业产能利用率环比上行，行业分化明显。工业产能利用率小幅上行至73.1%，二季
度为73%。分行业来看，采矿业整体小幅回升，但仍低于去年同期，其中煤炭开采和
洗选环比上行幅度较大， 石油和天然气开采业小幅回落。电力、 热力、 燃气及水生产
和供应业环比回升。制造业整体小幅回落， 上游制造业中， 非金属矿物制品、 化学原
料及化学制品业产能利用率环比修复， 有色、 黑色金属冶炼及压延产能利用率同环比
下滑。中游专用设备制造产能利用率环比上行，同比高于去年同期， 通用设备制造环
比放缓，但仍处于高位，电气机械和器材制造环比上行明显。下游制造业表现分化，
计算机、 通信和其他电子设备产能利用率由78.7%上行至80.2%， 领跑其他行业； 汽车
制造业同、环比下跌幅度较大，医药制造业也有所回落。

图表1：GDP同环比较二季度均有所抬升（%） 图表2：电子设备产能利用率持续上行（%）

2    工业生产回升，出口拉动作用较强 
季末月生产季节性回升， 出口拉动作用走强， 采矿业持续修复， 高技术产业生产高位
边际放缓。9月， 工业增加值当月同比增速上行0.3个百分点至5.5%，与市场预期的5.5%
一致，前三季度累计同比增速为5.3%。9月环比上涨0.61%，前值为0.54%。发电量同
比由上月的-0.8%回落至-1.2%。工业企业出口交货值当月同比由上月的11.1%上行至
17%，规模以上工业企业产销率当月同比由-0.1%下行为-0.2%。分三大门类看，9月，
采矿业同比上行2.7个百分点至1.3%，石油和天然气开采业本月同比上行幅度相对最
大，原煤产量同比跌幅收窄，天然原油产量同比上行，天然气产量同比由正转负。制
造业同比由上月的6.1%回落至6%； 高技术产业同比由上月的16.7%回落至15.3%。 电力、
燃气及水的生产和供应业同比上行0.7个百分点至5.6%。

图表3：采矿业持续修复，高技术产业高位放缓（%） 图表4：工业企业出口交货值增速攀升（亿元，%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

化工、 黑色金属行业生产上行， 下游制造业生产整体放缓， 消费品制造业整体回落。
细分行业来看， 上游制造业中，化学原料及化学制品制造业增速持续上行， 中东地缘
政治给供应链和原料价格带来较大扰动， 不确定性下企业补库需求提升， 乙烯、 化纤
产量上行。 非金属矿物制品、黑色金属冶炼及压延工业同比修复上行， 钢材、 水泥产
量同比跌幅收窄，极端天气影响消退后， 施工旺季带来需求回补， 政策性金融工具加
速落地， 叠加原材料成本上涨带来行业边际改善。 有色金属冶炼及压延工业、橡胶和
塑料制品业同比回落， 价格高位给生产带来一定抑制。中游制造业表现分化， 整体仍
处高位， 其中，通用设备、电气机械及器材制造业同比回落，专用设备制造业同比上
行。下游制造业中，汽车制造业同比回落2个百分点至6.7%，下滑幅度较大，高基数
带来一定影响；铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备制造业同比回落1.7个百分点
至11.7%，增速持续放缓。计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业同比回落，工业机器
人产量同比下滑幅度较大，集成电路产量同比下行，微型电子计算机产量跌幅收窄。
消费品制造业中，医药制造业同比回落0.3 个百分点至 4.9% ， 农副食品加工业、食品
制造业生产增速放缓，纺织业同比小幅上行。

3    投资：地产、基建延续磨底，制造业增速边际修复 
投资增速止跌企稳，资金投放和落地稳步推进，设备购置在政策推动下持续上行， 建
安工程继续下跌。2026年前三季度， 固定资产投资累计同比增速-7.2%，8月为-7.2%，
高于市场预期的-7.5%。固投季调环比增速为0.07%， 前值为-0.55%。随着稳增长政策
逐步落地， 固定资产投资逐步止跌企稳。其中， 房地产开发投资累计同比增速继续回
落0.7个百分点至-20.6%；基建累计同比增速回落0.2个百分点至-4.2%，制造业累计
同比增速上行0.2个百分点至-2.1%，民间固定资产投资回落0.3个百分点至-10.4%。
设备工器具购置同比增速由9.3%上行至9.7%，设备更新政策效果持续显现；建筑安装
工程同比由-9.8%回落为-10%，新开工偏弱，投资项目不足的影响仍存。第二产业投
资增速上行至主要支撑， 第三产业投资增速小幅回落， 第一产业下滑幅度较大。固投
资金来源来看，今年以来债券累计同比维持高位， 政府专项债用于项目建设比重明显
提升，预算内资金同比跌幅有所收窄，项目资金逐步落地。

3.1  房地产投资持续下滑，销售依然偏弱 
房地产投资增速持续下探，新开工、竣工跌幅加深。2026年9月，房地产投资累计同
比增速继续回落0.7个百分点至-20.6%，具体来看，房屋新开工面积累计同比增速回
落0.1个百分点至-24.9%，房屋施工面积累计同比持平于上月的-12.8%，竣工增速由
-23.7%回落为-25.1%。 
地产销售“金九”行情表现偏弱，二手房销售好于新房。9月，商品房销售面积累计
同比由-12.1%继续下探至-13.2%， 商品房销售额由-13%下跌为-13.9%， 房地产销售 “量”
的跌幅相对 “价” 更大。从70城房价数据来看， 一线城市新房和二手房销售价格环比
上涨， 二手房价格同比多数下跌，仅上海同比上行。 部分二线城市新房销售价格环比
上行， 三线城市新房、 二手房价格同环比持续放缓， 房价依然有待企稳。二手房交易
网签面积累计同比由10.6%小幅上行至10.7%， 二手房销售好于新房的格局持续， 但二
手房交易景气难以传导至房企回款与投资。商品房待售面积累计同比由-1.1%下跌至
-1.5%， 其中住宅累计同比由-0.5% 下行至 - 0.7% ， 地产库存持续去化。 8 月地产政策陆
续出台， 北京、 上海等核心城市购房调节进一步放松。 地产直至竣工验收具备交付条
件后方可收取购房资金和按揭贷款，优化了居民的购房环境，但从9月数据来看提振
效果相对有限。9月29日居民购房贷款贴息政策公布， 政策在10月1日期实施， 有望给

房企资金来源持续收紧， 销售回款和融资环境依然承压。房地产开发资金来源增速由
-21%回落为-21.6%。其中，个人按揭贷款跌幅小幅收窄，二手房交易活跃带来支撑，
定金及预收款增速与上月持平， 地产 “金九” 行情偏弱， 地产销售回款受限。 国内贷
款跌幅小幅修复上行， 自筹资金增速进一步下滑，地产行业信贷投放保持审慎， 房企
资金来源持续承压。 
房企拿地同比持续回落， 去库背景下， 房企依然偏谨慎。9月，100大中城市成交土地
占地面积环比季节性上行， 但同比由-16.1%继续小幅下调至-16.8%，房企拿地依然集
中在一线城市与核心城市的优质地块。房地产仍处去库进程当中，预售资金制度改革
影响将逐步显现， 房企资金周转受到约束， 叠加销售端和资金来源的限制，四季度土
地供应总量收缩的特征预期持续，结构逐步改善。

3.2  基建增速持续放缓，财政资金落地有望给行业带来托举

继续回落0.2个百分点至-4.2%， 三季度以来财政资金投放有所提速， 基建下行斜率明
显放缓， 但落地进程依然偏慢。 随着8000亿元政策性金融工具、 政府专项债和地方政
府债务结存限额在四季度投放并落地， “六张网” 建设进程有望提速， 四季度基建将

3.3  制造业投资反弹上行，高技术产业增速放缓 
制造业投资反弹上行，高技术产业增速边际放缓，出口和政策为主要拉动。9月，制
造业累计同比增速上行0.2个百分点至-2.1%，部分对冲了地产、 基建投资下行的压力，
民间固定资产投资回落0.3个百分点至-10.4%，依然表现偏弱；高技术产业累计增速
由5.2%回落至4.9%，设备工器具购置同比增速由9.3%上行至9.7%。 大规模设备更新政
策、 出口强韧性， 以及稳增长政策逐步落地给制造业带来拉动，上游资源品、 关键材
料等自主补库需求提高，也给制造业带来支撑。四季度随着财政资金投放陆续落地，
给制造业的支撑效果有望逐步显现。

4    消费增速修复，结构表现分化 
消费增速修复上行， “双节”带来提振，服务消费好转，线上消费放缓，内需整体依
然偏弱。9月， 社会消费品零售总额同比增速由上月的0.4%上行至1.1%， 市场预期0.7%，
累计同比持平于上月的1.1%。其中， 除汽车以外的消费品零售增速由上月的2.5%上涨
为3.9%， 上行幅度大于整体； 餐饮同比由上月的1.1%上涨为2.1%； 限额以上企业零售
同比由上月的-3.7%上涨为-2.1%。实物商品网上零售累计同比由4.3%回落为3.8%，网
上商品和服务占社零比重高位小幅下滑。城镇消费同比由0.2%上行至0.8%， 农村消费
由1.6%回升至2.7%，两者差距再度走扩。 
居民实际可支配收入边际上行， 人均消费支出持平，居民消费信心依然偏弱。2026年
前三季度， 全国居民人均可支配收入名义累计同比增长5.3%， 上半年为5.2%；扣除价
格因素实际累计同比4.4%， 前值为4.2%； 居民人均消费支出累计同比持平于上半年的
3%。从数据更新至8月的消费者信心指数来看，居民消费信心和意愿均小幅上行，但
收入信心指数持续回落。居民存款缓步回落， 就业和收入压力下， 信心有待进一步修
复。 
图表18：居民存款高位缓步回落（%） 图表19：消费者信心指数上行，收入信心指数回落

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

可选消费结构分化， 汽车为主要拖累， 地产下游消费偏弱， 必选消费表现稳健。可选
消费中，金银珠宝类本月跌幅收窄最为显著，黄金价格持续回落，高基数效应走弱，
给增速带来一定拉动。地缘政治影响下， 油价持续走高，在中秋和十一出行需求提振
下， 石油及制品类跌幅收窄。 汽车消费跌幅相对最大， 消费补贴和税费优惠政策退坡
带来一定影响。 地产下游行业中， 家电增速小幅放缓， 仍保持正增， 家具、 建材类跌
幅明显收窄。消费补贴对耐用品消费的透支作用逐步显现， 居民消费信心偏弱， 汽车、
地产后周期产品消费持续承压。 通讯器材类在高基数下， 增速持续走高， 新品发布周
期，内存价格大涨拉升了手机售价。必选消费增速均修复上行，其中粮油、食品类、
日用品类上行幅度较大。

5    失业率季节性回落，结构压力仍存 
失业率季节性回落， 农民工就业压力加大。9月，城镇调查失业率由5.3%下行至5.2%，
31个大城市城镇失业率也由5.3%下行至5.2%，外来农业户籍人口失业率持平于8月的
5.0%，BCI企业招工前瞻指数边际回落。9月就业人员平均工作时间由48.2小时/周上
行至48.3小时/周。从年龄结构来看，更新至8月的失业率数据中，16-24岁的年轻劳
动力失业率持续走高，25-29岁也有所上行，大学生毕业季带来的影响持续。随着大
学生就业逐步被吸纳，9月毕业季带来的影响季节性减弱。

6    总结与展望 
四季度政策将发挥托底作用，关注政策落地效果 。9月我国经济外需拉动、生产强、
内需弱的格局持续，但新旧经济分化程度边际收敛。9月稳增长政策逐步加码，政府
债发行提速， 在政策扶持下， 四季度经济有望缓步回升， 但仍需关注财政资金向实物
工作量的转化效率，预期全年经济节奏呈现“U型”走势。

7    风险提示 
地缘政治风险发展超预期，居民购房持续低迷，我国政策出台和落地不及预期。

## Document 197: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로긴 호흡으로 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로긴 호흡으로 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-29
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(005930) 
삼성전자 
2026.09.29

반도체 손인준 
6154 / sij@eugenefn.com

긴 호흡으로 
 투자의견 STRONG BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 560,000원(유지) 
 현재주가 270,000원(9/28)

 삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익이 103.6조원(+16%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 메모리 가격 등 전반적인 반도체 업황에 대한 기존 가정은 대체로 
유지했으나, 최근 원화 강세에 따라 원/달러 환율 가정을 1,440원에서 
1,400원으로 낮추고 MX 사업부의 적자 확대를 반영해 실적 추정치를 하
향 조정했다. 또한 2Q26 재고가 3Q26 매출로 인식되는 과정에서 발생하
는 추가 성과급 충당금 부담도 반영했다.  
 DS 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 104.0조원(+17%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 서버 DRAM과 eSSD 중심의 가격 인상이 이어지며, DRAM과 
NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +17%, +18%QoQ 상승할 것으로 추정한
다. 1cnm 수율 개선과 HBM4의 빠른 램프업도 실적 개선에 기여할 전망이
다. Foundry/LSI는 HBM4 베이스다이 생산 확대, Groq 3 LPU 양산 및 선
단 공정 가격 인상에 힘입어 하반기 매출 성장이 예상된다. 다만 성과급 충
당금과 감가상각비 부담으로 영업적자 축소 폭은 제한적일 것으로 보인다. 
 DX 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 -1.8조원(적자지속qoq)을 기록할 것으로 추
정한다. 삼성전자는 메모리 가격 급등에도 제품 가격 인상을 제한하며 시장 
점유율 확대에 주력하고 있다. 이러한 가격 정책과 갤럭시 Z 폴드8 등 신제
품 흥행에 힘입어 판매량은 당초 예상치를 상회할 것으로 보인다. 다만 원
가 상승분의 전가가 제한되면서 영업적자 폭은 크게 확대될 전망이다.  
 삼성전자의 4Q26 영업이익은 114.9조원(+11%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. DRAM과 NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +11%, +12%QoQ 상승
하며 견조한 흐름을 이어갈 것으로 보인다. 환율 가정 조정에 따라 2026년
과 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 각각 365.2조원(+735%yoy), 595.8조원
(+63%YoY)으로 낮췄다. 4Q26~1Q27 범용 DRAM·NAND 및 2027년 
HBM 가격 협상 환경은 공급업체에 유리하게 전개되고 있어, 향후 가격 가
정의 추가 상향 가능성이 높다고 판단한다.  
 극심한 DRAM 공급 부족으로 인한 HBM 적층 단수 하향 조정(base die 수
요 증가), Memory Wall에 대응하기 위한 3D Stacked DRAM·zHBM 도입 
논의 등은 파운드리의 기술적 가치를 부각시키는 요인이다. 파운드리의 수
익성 회복 속도는 완만할 전망이지만, 메모리와 파운드리를 연계한 턴키 비
즈니스의 시너지는 AI 반도체 시장 내 경쟁력을 높이고, 메모리 시장 점유
율 확대에도 기여할 것으로 보인다. 적극적인 메모리 가격 인상 정책으로 
업계 최고 수준의 Blended ASP도 장기간 유지할 것으로 예상한다. 
 이제 메모리 업종 주가는 업황의 지속 가능성을 확인하고 꾸준한 주주환원
을 반영하며, 중장기적으로 점진적인 상승세를 이어갈 것으로 판단한다. 삼
성전자는 메모리·파운드리를 아우르는 턴키 역량과 가격 정책에 기반한 실
적 차별화를 통해 피어 그룹 내 경쟁력을 높여갈 것으로 보인다.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다 
기다렸던 4 행정 중속 엔진 캐파 증설 단행 
HD현대중공업의 4행정 엔진 캐파는 기존 3.0GW에서 증설이 완료되는 '28년부터 
램프업을 거쳐 '30년 7.2GW까지 확대될 계획이다. ①[육상 발전용]은 신공장(울산 
온산) 3GW 와 HD 현대엔진(전남 영암) 1GW 를 합쳐 4.0GW, ②[선박용]은 
2.3GW에서 3.2GW로 확대된다. HD현대엔진 캐파는 기존 0.7GW에서 1GW까
지 확대되며, 해당 라인은 신공장이 가동되기 전 ‘27~’28 년 구간에서의 선박&육상 엔
진 모두 대응 가능하다. 신공장 캐파는 총 3.0GW로, ‘28년 약 30%의 가동률로 시
작해 램프업을 거쳐 ‘30 년부터 풀캐파로 20MW급 모델을 생산할 예정이다. 4 행정 
중속엔진에 대한 수요 가시성은 높다. 동사가 공유했던 육상 발전 엔진 인콰이어리 규
모는 기존 약 3~9GW였으나, 현재는 이를 크게 상회하는 수준으로 소통하고 있다.

최근 부유식 데이터센터(FDC)에 대한 하이퍼스케일러들의 인콰이어리 또한 확대되고 
있다. HD 한국조선해양은 최근 미국 선급(ABS)로부터 100MW급 규모의 부유식 발
전 설비(BMPP)에 대한 기본 인증(AiP)를 획득했으며, 동사는 FDC 신조 사업과 더불
어 발전원 4 행정 엔진을 파워십 및 부유식 발전설비(BMPP)에 납품하며 그룹사들과 
패키지 사업으로 진행할 예정이다. 가시화된 수요를 기반으로 엔진기계사업부의 구조적 
성장 보여줄 것으로 전망하며, 이에 따른 리레이팅을 예상한다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 및 목표주가 기존 97 만원 유지 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’ 및 목표주가 기존 97 만원을 유지한다. 동사는 
올해 상선수주 목표 이미 초과달성하며 안정적인 펀더멘털 유지 중이다. 하반기 남은 
기간은 LNGC, VLGC/VLAC 중심의 선별 수주 전략을 통해 ‘28 년 이후의 상선 부
문 수익성 유지 충분히 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 글로벌 지정학적 리스크 확대에 따른 
해군 현대화 사이클 기조에 맞추어 미 해군은 동맹국의 호위함 구매를 고려하고 있으
며, MASGA를 기반으로 중장기적으로 미국 전투함을 건조하기 위해 HD현대의 미국 
진출 가능성 높다는 판단이다. 또한 미국을 제외한 글로벌 함정(페루, 필리핀, 말레이시
아, 칠레, 사우디, 이집트 등) 수주 파이프라인도 다수 보유하고 있어 함정 모멘텀에 따
른 리레이팅 충분히 가능할 전망이다. 특수선+4 행정 엔진+FDC+SMR 등 조선소에 
붙일 수 있는 모든 추가 성장동력을 보유한 유일한 종목이기에 조선업 최선호주(Top-
Pick)로 제시한다. 최근 불거지고 있는 노조 파업 문제 해소될 시 저점 매수 기회라는 
판단이다.

2026-09-17

## Document 220: Advanced economies _ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Advanced economies  _ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
History tends to repeat itself in advanced economies. Once again, growth ultimately fell short of expectations by only a small
margin in the first half of 2026, despite the conflict in Iran. As early as 2025, the impact of tariffs was less severe than feared.
This is a sign that structural factors (notably AI, defence and electrification) are underpinning growth in these countries

and are expected to continue to do so in 2027. However, there are significant risks to the economic outlook, primarily of a
geopolitical nature, with a significant upside risk to inflation and interest rates. The other instance of history repeating itself
concerns a period that those under 20 cannot have experienced: the period leading up to the 2008 crisis, echoes of which can
be seen in terms of growth, inflation, the direction of monetary policy, and long-term interest rates.

Advanced economies are experiencing trends similar to those they
faced nearly 20 years ago:
- Growth remains resilient in 2025-26 despite shocks, as it did in 2006-
07, in the major advanced economies.
- Inflation is above central banks’ targets: as in 2006-07, this calls for a
tightening of monetary policy.
- In Europe and the United States, long-term interest rates have re -
turned to their 2006-07 levels (1996 in Japan): a challenging environ-

ment for public finances in a context where public debt ratios are signi-
ficantly higher than those seen 20 years ago.
THE GLOBAL ECONOMY IN THE MIDST OF A GROWTH CYCLE
We are already well into 2026, and the picture so far is clear: glo -
bal growth is proving resilient. Following a US tariff shock last year,
growth is once again proving, for the time being, to be significantly less
affected by the conflict in Iran than had been anticipated when it first

broke out. Initially, however, there were fears that this new shock would
hit growth even harder. As with the tariff hike in 2025, the impact was
measurable, particularly in terms of consumer confidence. But in both
2025 and 2026, growth has also shown a degree of resilience.
The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise
in oil prices, the recessionary impact of which is not yet apparent.
According to our forecasts, growth was expected to reach or exceed 1%

in 2026 in the four major advanced economies (the Eurozone, Japan,
the United Kingdom and the United States, where it exceeds 2%) for
the second consecutive year. This had not happened (excluding the
post-COVID catch-up period) since 2006-07. Growth figures are more
moderate (particularly for demographic reasons), but the private sec -
tor is not (at present) showing the same excesses (at that time, the
credit boom had preceded the worst crisis since the 1930s); although

debt levels have recently risen in the US tech sector.
The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial
intelligence (AI). It is fuelling global trade and business investment
in most countries. This is clearly the case in the United States, where
business investment is growing strongly as investment plans in the
US tech sector are implemented.
However, the world’s major economies are currently engaged in a
broader push to expand their capital goods investment. Alongside the

ongoing development of services—particularly digital services—we
have seen a rebound in industrial production in most countries since
early 2025. The leading sectors are primarily technology equipment,
machinery, aeronautics, defence and their essential inputs (energy and
metals). This trend is evident everywhere, including in Europe, and
is becoming entrenched over the long term, with an improvement in
business climate indicators, particularly new export orders. Growth is

therefore expected to strengthen further in 2027, particularly in the
Eurozone, where German investment plans are set to gather momen -
tum and whose spillover effects will have a greater impact on the eco-
nomy.
However, not all sectors are experiencing the same momentum. This
is particularly true for the sectors that depend on household consump-
tion, which has shown mixed trends. Our observation that consumer
spending is more buoyant in the United States than in Europe was

confirmed in 2026, and the outlook does not point to a change in this
ranking.
EDITORIAL
RESILIENCE TO SHOCK AFTER SHOCK

### Page 2

4
EcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
While European and US households are experiencing more or less the
same energy price shock, inflation remains higher in the United States.
But this has not prevented consumer spending from keeping pace, pri-
marily because it is driven by the wealthiest households, who are less
sensitive to inflation than to the wealth effect. Furthermore, while the

savings rate remains high in the Eurozone, US households have been
drawing down their savings. This is also true of British households,
which explains why the UK has seen slightly stronger growth since
the beginning of the year. In Japan, government spending has partially
offset energy inflation (while other countries are doing so to a much
lesser extent than in 2022), which has supported consumption growth.
However, these buffers—reduced savings or fiscal support—have their

limits when shocks persist. The two major current conflicts (Iran and
Ukraine) are far from being resolved, and their impact is continuing.
This persistent geopolitical risk and its implications for inflation repre-
sent a significant headwind to the growth outlook for 2027.
HIGHER INFLATION CALLS FOR MONETARY TIGHTENING
Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks
examined in this publication, which is another similarity (albeit with

different underlying causes) with the 2006-07 period. Japan is the ex -
ception, as government support measures for households are tempo -
rarily holding back inflation.
Apart from the Bank of Japan, which is engaged in a long-term norma-
lisation process, no central bank was expected to raise its key rates
in 2026. But they are now being prompted to do so for reasons that
vary from one region to another. The first is the conflict in Iran and
the resulting rise in oil prices, which triggered a rebound in energy

inflation from the second quarter onwards. However, since the conflict
was not resolved by the memorandum of understanding signed in June,
and given that the subsequent drop in oil prices proved short-lived,
inflation is expected to rise again. The rise in gas prices is expected to
exacerbate the impact, particularly in Europe, where inventories are
being replenished ahead of winter. Electricity prices are expected to
follow suit in most countries, but at a more moderate pace than in

2022. Indeed, gas is now used less to generate electricity than it was
at that time (thanks to the expansion of renewable energy), and to an
even lesser extent in France.
There is a significant risk that energy inflation will spread further to
other components of inflation in the future, and inflation expectations
could eventually reflect this. This is why the European Central Bank
has already raised its key interest rate twice (in June and September)

and, according to our scenario, will likely do so again in December. For
the same reason, we expect the Bank of England to raise its policy rate
in November. Although the cap on energy prices was maintained for
a long time, it has now been raised. As a result, inflation is expected
to rise between now and the end of the year. The aim will then be to
prevent second-round effects.
Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength. In the

United States, it has exceeded the 2% target since 2021. The main
argument in favour of a new cycle of rate hikes (following the one in
2022-2023) lies in the current resilience of growth and the improving
labour market. The Federal Reserve (Fed) must balance two equally
important objectives: maximum employment and price stability. Howe-
ver, unlike in the 4 th quarter of 2025, when the decline in job crea -
tion justified monetary easing (by 75 basis points), the current sharp

rebound in job creation completely reverses that analysis. According
to our forecasts, the unemployment rate is expected to decrease in
the coming months, which pushes back the prospect of a decline in
core inflation. This situation persists due to both supply constraints
(over which a central bank has no control) and excess demand (which
the Fed can, however, mitigate). Consequently, the Fed raised its key
rate in September and is expected to implement two further rate hikes

between now and January 2027.
In Japan, finally, while headline inflation has been brought down by
government support measures, core inflation remains significant.
Wage pressures are mounting against a backdrop of labour shortages.
The Bank of Japan is therefore being forced to accelerate the pace of its
monetary normalisation.
In all these regions, inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026
or Q1 2027. It is against this backdrop of temporary inflation that rate

hikes could also come to an end by that time (excluding Japan). Howe-
ver, this assumes that once the energy shock—the course of which
remains uncertain—subsides, inflation will fall. The effects of El Niño,
which are still difficult to gauge at present, could take over. Inflation
would then remain above the central banks’ target for a longer period.
Under this scenario, rate hikes would go further than is currently en -
visaged.
THE RISE IN LONG- TERM INTEREST RATES: A FACTOR THAT WILL WEIGH

ON PUBLIC FINANCES
Monetary policies have also to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal
policy, particularly in the United States (where the budget deficit is
high and shows no signs of shrinking) and in Japan (where the new
government is pursuing pro-growth policies). In Europe, fiscal policy
is playing a much smaller role than it did in 2022. While the stimulus
measures implemented at that time did help reduce the inflation rate
in the short term (by nearly 2.5 pp in France, for example), by keeping

demand at a high level, they delayed disinflation and increased the
scale of the monetary tightening required.
The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: it triggered a
rise in long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the
start of the year) to levels close to those of 2006-07. This rise is wides-
pread, but its causes vary. Inflation expectations appear to play a more
significant role in the Eurozone, whereas the rise in real interest rates

seems to be the main factor in the United States and Japan.

The bank
for a changing
world
Higher inflation and more resilient real growth than feared point in
the same direction: stronger than expected nominal growth that is li -
kely to be sustained. However, in the long term, high nominal growth
tends to drive up nominal interest rates (as it is a positive sign of more
profitable investment projects), in contrast to the problem of secular

stagnation raised in the 2010s (which combined low nominal growth
with low nominal interest rates).
The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period
of growth), with various situations. In several countries, the public
debt-to-GDP ratio continues to rise. In the United States, deficit reduc-
tion does not appear to be a priority, to the extent that it is expected
to remain above 6% of GDP at the very least until the end of Donald

Trump’s term of office (with little hope that the mid-term elections will
significantly alter this trend). In the United Kingdom and France, fiscal
consolidation has begun, but it is being hampered by the simultaneous
increase in certain areas of expenditure (debt servicing, defence and
healthcare, due in particular to an ageing population), which is slowing
the pace of consolidation. In Germany, the implementation of invest -
ment plans is leading to a widening of the deficit. While this does not

pose a sustainability problem, it does result in an increase in annual
debt issuance. In Japan, the budget deficit is kept in check by interest
payments that remain low, as the rise in interest rates is recent and
the average maturity of the debt is high (10 years). However, expansio-
nary fiscal policy could pose further problems in terms of debt dyna -
mics if it were to continue through the end of the decade. In Italy and
Spain, fiscal consolidation has been sufficient to stabilise the public

debt-to-GDP ratio (and even to reduce it in Spain), but the high level of
Italy’s debt service it vulnerable to a rise in interest rates.
In all these countries, the fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it
will be in 2030. Indeed, in most cases, only half of the debt was is -
sued after 2022 (that is, after the end of the zero-interest-rate period).
The debt service has so far only partially risen, except in the United
States, where most of the increase has already occurred due to a shor-

ter average debt maturity (6 years). This means that, for all countries
combined, the rate of GDP growth remains higher than the average
interest rate paid on public debt (or the effective interest rate).
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with
GDP growth. As a result, in most cases, the debt service will have re -
turned to the level as a share of GDP seen in the early 2000s. In order to
stabilise public debt-to-GDP ratios, primary balances will need to have

improved. This will even require the generation of primary surpluses,
particularly in Italy (which is already achieving this), France and the
United Kingdom. This is a crucial shift that governments will need to
introduce as early as the 2027 budget; otherwise, they risk postponing
the stabilisation of their public debt-to-GDP ratio once again.
Article completed on 17 September 2026
Stéphane Colliac
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

## Document 230: AI崩了，法债爆了，急需美债“避险”！10年期美债收益率创“硅谷银行危机”来最大跌幅

### Page 1

⾼盛分析师指出， AI 资本⽀出回撤意味着债券潜在供给减少，效果近似量化宽松。叠加法
国主权债务危机加速恶化与中东油价飙升，推动避险资⾦流⼊美债。 10 年期美债收益率单
⽇下⾏ 19.3 个基点重回 5% 以下，为 2023 年 3 ⽉ 10 ⽇硅⾕银⾏危机以来最⼤单⽇跌幅。
摘要：
AI 科技股的⼀轮集中抛售，意外成为美债市场三年来最猛烈的单⽇反弹的导⽕索。
周四， 10 年期美债收益率单⽇下⾏ 19.3 个基点⾄ 4.952% 重回 5% 以下，为 2023 年 3 ⽉ 10 ⽇硅⾕银
⾏危机以来最⼤单⽇跌幅。据 Dow Jones Market Data ，过去⼗年仅有 15 个交易⽇出现过如此幅
度的单⽇波动，涵盖 2020 年新冠危机、 2023 年区域银⾏危机、 2025 年 4 ⽉关税冲击以及今年 2 ⽉
美以对伊朗发动战争等重⼤事件。
2 年期收益率下⾏ 18.8 个基点⾄ 4.635% ，为 2025 年 8 ⽉ 1 ⽇以来最⼤单⽇跌幅； 30 年期收益率下
⾏ 15.1 个基点⾄ 5.286% ，为 2024 年 8 ⽉以来最⼤单⽇跌幅。 5 年期和 7 年期美债当天甚⾄下跌了

20 个基点。
PGIM Credit 全球债券主管 Robert Tipp 说： “中东局势升级、法国问题持续恶化以及 AI 领域疑虑
上升 —— 你看到的是多重压⼒同时爆发。”
他补充道： “正常交易⽇，⼀次像样的波动也就⼏个基点。今天你看到的是三倍于此。”
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

三重压⼒同时引爆
Mackenzie Investments ⾸席固收策略师 Dustin Reid 说： “这种级别的波动，通常意味着某种恐
慌或危机正在发⽣。 ”
他的判断是： “我们正处于⼀个围绕 AI 过度扩张的主题之中。” 这触发了投资者开始 “ 落袋为安 ” ，资
⾦从股票轮转⾄美债和固收市场。
具体来看，三条线索同时触发：
其⼀， AI 财报季的 “ 不及预期 ” 。 Lam Research 业绩虽强但未突破隐性预期上限，指引⽆明显上
⾏空间；特斯拉将全年资本⽀出指引从逾 250 亿美元下调⾄约 200 亿美元； Meta 将全年资本⽀出
指引下限上调⾄ 1350 亿美元，但这意味着仅第四季度单季⽀出即需达到 490 亿⾄ 590 亿美元，市
场对执⾏可⾏性产⽣疑虑。 Meta 已与亚⻢逊⼀道进⼊⾃由现⾦流为负的阵营。
其⼆，法国主权债务危机加剧。 法国 10 年期国债收益率盘中⼀度升⾄ 5.142% ，为 2002 年 6 ⽉以
来最⾼盘中⽔平，收盘报 5.082% 。法国与德国 10 年期国债利差扩⼤⾄逾 170 个基点，创 2011 年

欧债危机以来最⾼；更值得注意的是，法国 10 年期收益率⼀度⾼于意⼤利同期限收益率约 50 个
基点 —— 历史上从未出现过这⼀情形。 法国 CDS 超过 100 个基点，为 2012 年以来⾸次。
其三，油价飙升叠加中东紧张局势。 胡塞武装对沙特发动新⼀轮袭击，沙特阿美沙伊巴赫油⽥
遭⽆⼈机袭击， WTI 原油价格逼近每桶 100 美元的伊朗战争⾼点。布伦特原油重上每桶 112 美
元。
DWS 美洲固收主管 George Catrambone 表⽰，尽管上⽉美债收益率⼤幅上⾏， “ 美债仍具备避险
资产属性 ” ，法国的动荡 “ 带来了⼀定的避险买盘 ” 。
AI 资本⽀出退潮，债市的 “ 另类宽松 ”
⾼盛 Rich Privorotsky 在给机构客⼾的晨报中将这⼀现象定性为 “ 令⼈瞩⽬的反⾝性 ” ：
逻辑链条清晰：超⼤规模云计算企业发⾏债务的规模已可与美国国债相提并论， AI 资本⽀出的任
何收缩，实质上等同于减少债券市场的潜在供给压⼒。
AI 疲软重新定价了资本⽀出周期，减轻了潜在债券发⾏的压⼒，并⽇益演变为⼀次增⻓冲击。反

⾝性之强令⼈瞩⽬ —— 正是制造股票问题的那件事，正在帮助解决利率问题。

### Page 3

Meta 的案例最具说明意义。巴克莱信⽤分析师 Andrew Keches 在财报后报告中写道：
换⾔之， Meta 现在既需要债市，也需要股市，⽽且两者都需要⼤量。
数据中⼼ CDS 利差重新飙升⾄历史新⾼；超⼤规模云计算企业整体信⽤⻛险持续攀升，并开始
向整个信⽤市场蔓延 —— 欧元区⾼收益信⽤违约互换指数 iTraxx Europe Crossover 升⾄ 329 个
基点，为近七个⽉最⾼⽔平。
⾼盛： CTA 空头回补 “ 空间还很⼤ ”
这轮美债涨势背后， CTA 空头回补是重要推⼿之⼀。⾼盛期货交易台数据显⽰，趋势跟踪量化
基⾦⽬前已是⾼盛所追踪的每个债券市场的净买家。
本周预计买⼊ 1800 万美元 DV01 ，若收益率持续下⾏可升⾄ 2700 万；⼀个⽉维度基准买⼊规模
将达 5500 万美元 DV01 ，但这仅约为其模拟净空仓规模的三分之⼀ —— 空头回补压⼒仍有相当⼤
的释放空间。
我们对此的热情，在⼀定程度上被 14C ⽂件中暗⽰的庞⼤⽀出野⼼所削弱。我们的判断是，

Meta 未来将同时依赖信贷市场和股票市场，⽽债券发⾏的绝对规模也可能上升。我们继续预计
近期将有债券发⾏，这将在⼀段时间内压制信⽤利差。

与此同时， CME 未平仓合约数据显⽰，⻓债期货此前积累了⼤规模新增空头头⼨，这批空头在
美债上涨过程中⾯临被动回补压⼒，进⼀步助推了涨势。
期权市场：押注收益率继续下⾏
据彭博报道，美债期权对冲成本周四飙升⾄ 3 ⽉以来最⾼⽔平。
⼀项衡量指标显⽰，市场参与者为⻓期美债看涨期权⽀付的溢价⾼于看跌期权，为七个⽉来⾸
次 —— 这意味着投资者正在为债券进⼀步上涨做准备。
当⽇最引⼈注⽬的头⼨之⼀：⼀名交易员斥资约 700 万美元期权费，在上午交易时段建⽴起约
6.5 万张合约的头⼨，押注 10 年期收益率在⼀个⽉内降⾄ 4.6% 附近。
股市： AI 资本⽀出叙事全⾯动摇
纳斯达克指数当⽇跌 2.6% ，费城半导体指数跌超 7% ，英特尔、 AMD 跌超 8% ，⾼通跌超 3% 。标
普 500 指数跌 1.5% ，但等权重标普 500 当⽇相对标普 500 录得 2024 年 7 ⽉以来最佳单⽇表现 ——
防御性板块医疗、公⽤事业逆势⾛强。

略下跌 1.4%—— 这是⾃新冠疫情以来三类策略⾸次在同⼀天均跌超 1% 。对冲基⾦重仓多空组合
（ GSTHVIPP ）当⽇下跌约 4.2% ，为 2011 年以来最⼤单⽇跌幅，本⽉累计跌幅达 10.4% 。

胁。这对⻛险资产⽽⾔，是⼀个⽐指数跌幅本⾝更令⼈担忧的信号。

加息的局⾯。 ”

抛售近期 AI 赢家、买⼊医疗、消费和公⽤事业等 ' ⽆聊即美 ' 板块的浪潮。

## Document 286: El Niño 2026–2027_ multiple pressures on prices and supplies (2026-09-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/El Niño 2026–2027_ multiple pressures on prices and supplies (2026-09-30).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

30 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
EL NIÑO 2026–2027: MULTIPLE PRESSURES ON PRICES AND SUPPLIES
Hélène Drouot
The 2026–2027 El Niño climate event is expected to be one of the most intense in the last fifty years, with sea-surface-temperature
anomalies potentially exceeding +3°C in the equatorial Pacific. According to the World Meteorological Organisation (WMO), El Niño
is expected to persist until February 2027, with asymmetrical effects, as South-East Asia and northern Latin America will experience

droughts, while southern Brazil and Argentina could see heavy rainfall. Above all, El Niño is compounding the effects of global warming
and exacerbating existing extreme-weather events (droughts, floods and heatwaves). The economic effects of this climate shock mainly
play out through three avenues: agricultural commodity prices, energy and logistics (see heatmap).
Agricultural commodity prices: volatility is causing tensions, with inflationary effects

In India and South-East Asia, droughts will affect harvests, pushing up prices (particularly for coffee, cocoa, rice and palm oil). Conversely, in
southern Brazil and Argentina, heavy rainfall will boost soya and maize production, putting downward pressure on prices. According to the IMF,
El Niño causes global food prices to rise by between 5% (in a typical episode) and 8% (very high intensity) year-on-year, with the full impact being
felt 6 to 12 months after the climatic peak.

Therefore, for a given country, the weighting of food in its inflation index (CPI) plays a decisive role. As a result, all other things being equal, an 8%
rise in prices adds around 1 pp to inflation when food accounts for 13% of the CPI basket (average for countries shown in green on the heatmap),
1.8 pp when this share reaches 22% (yellow), 2.8 pp at 35% (orange) and nearly 4 pp above 49% (red).
The risks are skewed to the upside, as the 8% estimate does not take into account additional losses linked to droughts, floods and other extre -

me events, while policy responses to shocks (stockpiling by importers and increased export restrictions) may amplify and prolong price pres -
sures. The rice market between 2023 and 2024 illustrates this well, as the combination of rice-export restrictions imposed by India in July 2023
(in order to protect its domestic market) and of the drought in South-East Asia contributed to a 30% average increase in the price of rice over the
course of 2023 and kept global prices high for several months after the end of the El Niño episode.

Energy production sector: heavy reliance on hydroelectricity leaves the sector vulnerable to a second supply shock
Droughts will reduce hydroelectric power generation in Latin America and South-East Asia. As a result, some countries will have to turn to other,
more expensive solutions, such as fossil fuels, against a backdrop of already high oil and gas prices. This shift will increase global demand for
oil and put pressure on prices which, according to the IMF, could persist for up to four quarters after the initial El Niño shock. For example,

in Colombia, where hydropower accounts for 55% of electricity generation, a 30% drop in output (already seen in 2023–2024) could increase the
cost of electricity generation by 15 to 20% (assuming that the cost of thermal power generation is twice as high as hydropower). In Ecuador (where
hydropower accounts for 70% of electricity generation) and Vietnam (30%), electricity shortages are likely to disrupt local industries.
SOURCE: IMF, WORLD BANK, EMBER, BNP PARIBAS CALCULATIONS

Vulnerabilities differ, depending on a country’s dependence on food, energy and logistics

Reading note: Scores are standardised on a scale of 0 to 10 for ease of comparison between countries and supply channels.
- Food vulnerability: based on the weight of food in the CPI.
- Energy vulnerability: based on the share of hydropower in electricity generation.
- Logistics vulnerability: the ‘Trade and transport-related infrastructure’ component of the World Bank’s Logistics Perfor-

mance Index, which measures the quality of the infrastructure required for transport and international trade.

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 30 September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
Logistics: transport infrastructure is another point of vulnerability
The final channel relates to disruptions to freight transport against a backdrop of already weakened value chains. The variable used in our
heatmap is a proxy for economies’ abilities to absorb a shock (particularly a climate-related one) affecting their transport infrastructure (for

example, by maintaining traffic flow, diverting or reorganising transport flows). The heatmap highlights the particularly high vulnerability
of Latin America. Its capacity to absorb a logistics shock is relatively limited, and there is significant exposure at a strategic chokepoint, the
Panama Canal, through which approximately 5% of global trade passes. Low rainfall in the canal basin has already led to the number of
daily transits being reduced in September 2026 (as in 2023–2024); further restrictions are expected in October (to 29 transits per day, down

from 36 in August), which will increase transport times and costs. Europe is also being affected, with low water levels on the Rhine and the
Danube already disrupting river transport and electricity generation.
Latin America and South-East Asia are hit hardest
Therefore, the economies most at risk are not necessarily those that will suffer the greatest climate shock, but those where multiple trans -
mission channels converge, including a high weighting of food prices in inflation, reliance on hydroelectricity, fragile transport infrastructure

and limited fiscal room for manoeuvre. Latin America and South-East Asia are home to some of the most vulnerable countries, particularly
due to their dependence on food prices and hydroelectricity. Colombia and Ecuador are noteworthy for their high exposure to energy risk,
while Ghana, India and the Philippines are some of the economies most vulnerable to a food shock. Latin America faces a combination of
vulnerabilities, such as high exposure to food and energy price shocks in some countries, which are compounded by logistics risks, as currently

illustrated by the restrictions on traffic through the Panama Canal.
helene.drouot@bnpparibas.com

until 2027“

Are investors rational?
The stock market strength and resilience over the last 3 months appears to be a rational response to strong
earnings and only limited signs that higher energy prices are materially affecting broad corporate profits.
Our dbLumina gauge indicates investors have been irrational during market

drops and periods of geopolitical tensions What does it mean to be ‘rational’?
Risk-off periods:
The tariff fears in 2025 and then the Iran
war in 2026 were key buying opportunities
in equity markets. Yet, the overwhelming
fear exhibited by investors in these periods
largely meant they sold off risk assets in an
emotional and irrational response.
Risk-on periods:
It can be rational to buy into a rising
market. Our indicator suggests this is what
happened in Q3 as earnings growth

continued to be strong.
Fear Indicators:
Sharp, disproportionate reactions to
threats, widespread sell-offs on
announcements, panic, flight to safety
beyond what might be fundamentally
warranted.

Emerging risks underestimated by the market – part 1
A reallocation of global sovereign reserve assets is accelerating, spurred by
geopolitical uncertainty and the fear of trade and financial sanctions. Notably,
central banks are repatriating their physical assets, such as the Dutch and French

central banks which have recently moved gold reserves out of New York.
Furthermore, a World Gold Council survey reveals that 45% of global central banks
plan to increase their gold reserves over the next 12 months.
What the market underestimates: The market views the recent surges in gold prices
as temporary, cyclical safe-haven flows. In reality, sovereign institutions are
structurally preparing for a more fragmented global trade system where tariff

policies and sanctions may make dollar-clearing assets more difficult.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Interest rates: At a time when the "world is awash in debt", long-term interest rates
of some countries may be forced structurally higher to attract alternative private
capital.
Inflation: Unexpected dollar depreciation may increase the cost of imported goods
Equity Prices: High-growth US equity valuations, which are highly sensitive to the

long-term risk-free rate, may face multiple compression if bond yields are pushed
higher.
The market currently prices in a material, long-term GDP boost from AI-driven
productivity gains. However, some research from the IMF suggests that over-reliance
on standardised LLMs can produce a high degree of "sameness" and uniformity in
cognitive output. Furthermore, escalating intellectual property and copyright
litigation threaten to bifurcate the quality of AI models based strictly on developers'

licensing budgets.
What the market underestimates: Some investors assume that the exponential
curve of AI hardware capabilities translates directly into exponential economic
output. But there is a risk that if AI tools merely standardise corporate processes
rather than innovate them, and legal barriers restrict access to premium data, the
productivity boom may be less aggressive than expected.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
GDP growth: Structural growth ceiling if AI fails to deliver the multi-percentage

point boost to global productivity required to offset aging demographics.
Equity prices: A downward revaluation of the technology and software sector if
massive AI infrastructure expenditures fail to generate revenue according to existing
expectations.
Geopolitical fragmentation of global reserve assets The AI “sameness“ trap and the productivity stagnation1 2
8

### Page 10

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging risks underestimated by the market – part 2
Advanced economies are facing a dual demographic crisis: rapidly aging populations
and underfunded pension systems. This is forcing some older people to remain in or
re-enter the workforce out of financial necessity rather than choice.

What the market underestimates: Lower unemployment rates are celebrated as a
sign of economic strength. However, in structurally ageing societies like Japan and
parts of Europe, the growth of the labour force is increasingly driven by part-time,
older workers. Furthermore, G20 nations lack adequate, cohesive policy frameworks
to address these long-term demographic fiscal drains.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Inflation: An older workforce working out of necessity has less discretionary

spending power, potentially locking economies into persistent, structural demand-
side deflationary forces, offset by supply-side wage pressures in highly skilled
sectors.
GDP growth: Labour productivity growth that is lower than it might otherwise be as
the median age of the workforce climbs, limiting long-term potential GDP.
The geopolitical battleground is focussed on energy blockades (such as the Strait of
Hormuz) but underestimates the weaponisation of critical minerals. China currently

holds ‘escalation dominance’ over rare-earth mineral supply chains. The emerging
risk is the proactive restriction of these minerals to Western firms, such as recent
bans on supplying rare-earth inputs to some US firms.
What the market underestimates: The market treats rare-earth supply constraints as
temporary trade spat negotiation tactics. In reality, as Western nations aggressively
push for reshoring and friend-shoring, China is increasingly using its processing

monopoly as a long-term strategic blockade to try to slow Western high-tech,
defence and the energy transition.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Inflation: Upwards pressure on inflation as the cost of manufacturing electric
vehicles, semiconductors, and advanced electronics surges due to supply blockades
and the high capital cost of rebuilding localised processing facilities.
Interest rates: Central banks may be forced to keep policy rates higher to combat

the persistent supply-side inflation generated by supply-chain fragmentation.
The fragmentation of the EU’s political centre Critical mineral isolationism and ‘green’ supply chain blockades3 4
9

### Page 11

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging themes – part 1
Our dbLumina AI model helps us identify the key emerging themes that have the potential to
most impact markets and economies in the short term
While the previous focus was on the AI hardware squeeze and
commercial competition, the narrative is shifting towards AI's role as a

critical component of national security and geopolitical leverage.
— AI access: We see this in President Trump's framing of AI development as a
geopolitical race ("whoever wins AI wins").
— AI as a bargaining chip: Several examples over the last 12 months including the
2025 deal whereby AMD agreed to pay 15% of sales from certain AI chips to the
US government in exchange for an export license. Even if these deals change, the
trajectory is one where access to AI technology is tied to national economic and

security interests.
— Direct military & security applications: Several examples including, cyberattacks
on critical infrastructure (such as a UK power plant), the use of AI in developing
"exceptional" phages to fight E.coli, and the growing field of AI security
discussions.
Outlook: Expect more government intervention, the use of AI technology access as a
tool in diplomatic negotiations, and a greater focus on the dual-use nature of AI in
both commercial and military contexts. This will likely create regulatory and

operational uncertainty for tech firms.
The trend of reshoring is evolving into a more proactive diplomatic
strategy where nations are forming new, often transactional, alliances
to navigate a world less dominated by a single superpower.
— Proactive alliance building: Canadian Prime Minister Carney has been front and
centre making his first international trip to France and the UK in an effort to
diversify away from traditional reliance on the US. That follows other key

meetings between Japan and India, and more.
— Bypassing traditional powers: Greenland signalled its willingness to turn to
China for investment if Western countries do not provide support, reinforcing the
leverage of some smaller nations.
— Shifting security arrangements: The US pushed for a ‘reassurance force’ in
Ukraine, while French President Macron hosted a "coalition of the willing" to
discuss Europe's defence without US guarantees. Further examples include the

discussion of a Franco-German "nuclear sharing" arrangement.
Outlook: The assumptions of a bipolar world order is giving way to a more complex,
multi-nodal system. This will create greater emphasis on regional blocs and issue-
specific coalitions. The stability of traditional alliances like NATO and the G7 remain
deeply uncertain.
AI’s impact on geopolitics and national security The fracturing of multilateralism and the rise of "Middle Power Countries" 1 2

10

### Page 12

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging themes – part 2
These four key themes dbLumina identifies are sometimes ‘known’ by investors, however, they may be underestimated by the market,
particularly when there are some potentially series longer-term impacts
Previous talk of “sovereign fiscal fragility” has now manifested itself

into a severe market reaction. Although fears around government debt
and deficits pop up from time to time, the magnitude of September’s
bond sell-off has been compounded by oil price concerns
— Accelerating sovereign sell-off: Concerns about inflation and debt sustainability
are not new but the peaks in bond yields are. Yields on 10-year Treasuries, bunds,
and gilts all rose sharply, with bunds hitting their highest level since 2009.
— Fiscal policy as a direct market catalyst: The market is increasingly sensitive to

fiscal policy announcements. For example, in the UK postponed tax rises, led to a
spike in gilt yields. Similarly, France's political turmoil and concerns over its
budget deficit caused the Franco-German yield spread to widen significantly.
— The “awash in debt" narrative: Treasury Secretary Bessent's comment at the G20
that "The world is awash in debt" signalled that the issue is increasingly top-of-
mind for policymakers.
Outlook: Markets will be increasingly quick to punish any perceived fiscal

irresponsibility. Expect higher borrowing costs, currency volatility, and increased risk
of financial instability, especially for countries with high debt levels.
The strain in the US consumer and labour markets is now diverging
sharply from the health of large corporations, reinforcing the K-shaped
economy.
— Strong corporate performance: Earnings season for Q2 showed remarkably solid
global growth, and the resilience of large corporations was a recurring theme

that helped support equity markets despite other headwinds.
— Weak consumer & labour signals: Economic reports have showed some signs of
a weakening labour market, falling consumer confidence, and stress among low-
income Americans. The New York Fed's survey also showed households expect a
worse financial situation.
— Policy and market disconnect: While strong corporate performance depends on
a robust economy, the underlying consumer weakness is concerning. Expect the

market to continue its twitchy reaction to data, as investors struggle to reconcile
these conflicting signals.
Outlook: This theme is potentially the most consequential for the domestic US
economy. If corporate profits and equity markets "detach" from the real economy as
consumers perceive it, it could lead to significant social and political instability,
making economic policy even more difficult to navigate.
The return of global bond vigilantes The bifurcation of economies: corporate resilience vs. consumer strain3 4

## Document 415: “共识交易”的瓦解和新共识的诞生！高盛解答：为何机构当下都在大幅减仓AI

Source PDF: all report pdf/“共识交易”的瓦解和新共识的诞生！高盛解答：为何机构当下都在大幅减仓AI.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-24
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

" 做多 AI 、做空⼀切 " 的极度拥挤交易正在痛苦解构 —— 市场不再满⾜于 " 烧钱叙事 " ，超⼤规
模云⼚商必须在 1.1 万亿美元资本⽀出的同时，拿出真实可⻅的收⼊转化。 40-50 吉⽡的电
⼒缺⼝、资⾦成本攀升与 " ⽀出过剩 " 时代的宏观范式切换，正将 AI 交易从信仰题变成⼀道严
酷的变现⼤考。
摘要：
此前市场极度拥挤的 “ 做多 AI ，做空其他⼀切 ” 交易正在经历痛苦的解构，创纪录抛售下，超⼤规
模云⼚商迎 “ 变现⼤考 ” 。
10 ⽉ 22 ⽇，⾼盛欧洲固定收益与股票（ FICC & Equities ）交易主管 Rich Privorotsky 在位于伦敦
的⾼盛交易室接受了播客栏⽬《 The Markets 》的访谈。 Privorotsky 透露了⼀个极具冲击⼒的数
据：“ 上周我们的主经纪商业务账簿（ Prime book ）上，出现了有史以来最⼤规模的科技股抛
售。 ”
⾯对这波抛售潮，正在进⾏中的三季度财报季显得尤为关键。市场的焦点已从单纯的 “ 讲故事 ” 转

向了严苛的业绩兑现。 Privorotsky 指出，市场预计明年 AI 的资本⽀出（ Capex ）将⾼达 1.1 万亿
美元，但这已不⾜以让投资者买单。
“ 仅 仅 说 你 花 了 很 多 钱 是 不 够 的 ， 你 还 需 要 证 明 未 完 成 的 订 单 正 在 转 化 为 实 质 性 的 收
⼊。 ”Privorotsky 强调， “ 他们必须在推动资本⽀出的同时展⽰这些⽀出的回报。这不是⼀个轻松
的局⾯。 ” 他表⽰，云业务的增⻓、变现能⼒以及⾃由现⾦流实现收⽀平衡的速度，将对⽬前极其
敏感的信⽤市场产⽣重⼤影响。
基础设施与资⾦双重掣肘：明年存在 “40-50 吉⽡ ” 电⼒缺⼝
在 AI 狂热降温的背后，是现实世界中基础设施和资⾦池的硬性约束。市场⽬前正在与三个核⼼问
题作⽃争，⾸当其冲的便是资⾦成本的溢出效应和电⼒供应。
Privorotsky 指出，杠杆率较⾼的 AI 交易领域（如新兴的云服务提供商）正⾯临严峻的融资考
验： “ 这些企业在寻找融资来兑现 AI ⽀出时是否会遇到困难？ ”

更现实的物理瓶颈在于电⼒交付能⼒。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

他提出了⼀组关键数据： “ 明年的电⼒缺⼝⼤约在 40 到 50 吉⽡。如果你⽆法获得实际为数据中⼼
供电的能源，你还能消耗掉你已经提前订购的芯⽚吗？ ” 这些担忧导致投资者进⼊ “ 去⻛险模
式 ” ，通过做空 AI 和 AI 资本⽀出交易来降低增⻓预期，同时资⾦涌⼊债券市场避险。
宏观范式重塑：从 “ 储蓄过剩 ” ⾛向 “ ⽀出过剩 ”
站在宏观周期的⻆度， Privorotsky 描绘了⼀幅深刻的范式转换图景。
他表⽰，⾃⼰的整个职业⽣涯都处于 “ 储蓄过剩 ” 的环境中 —— 系统⾥的钱多到不知道该怎么办。
但现在，情况发⽣了 “ 地震般的剧变（ Seismic shift ） ” 。
“ 在过去的⼀年⾥，我们预计将在 AI 资本⽀出上花费超过 1 万亿美元，⽽全球⼤多数政府都在运⾏
财政⾚字。我们正处于⼀个 ‘ ⽀出过剩 ’ 的时代。 ”
这种转变推⾼了资本成本，并⾃然⽽然地挤出了处于边缘、资质较差的借款⼈。
他观察到，这种压⼒不仅体现在 CCC 级信⽤利差的⼤幅⾛阔上，甚⾄蔓延到了主权债务领域

（如⾯临⾼额财政⾚字的法国国债）。再加上居⾼不下的能源价格，整体市场的⻛险偏好正在被
显著削弱。
市场情绪⾛向两极， “ 成也 AI ，败也 AI”
在复杂的宏观和微观交织下，投资者的焦虑情绪出现了奇特的 “ 极化 ” 现象。
Privorotsky 分享了他本周与投资者交流的⻅闻： “ 我遇到⼀些投资者说， ‘ 我担⼼⽆法交付算⼒，
这让我害怕 ’ ；然后我⼜遇到了另⼀批投资者，他们说， ‘ 我担⼼我们会过度交付算⼒，这让我害
怕 ’ 。不知何故，两个完全互斥的结果构成了市场担忧的总和。 ”
由于能源价格对经济产⽣重⼤影响（标普 500 虽然表现平稳，但中位数股票实际上已下跌超过
10% ），⾦融状况指数（ FCI ）⼤幅收紧，迫使美联储采取更多⾏动。
Privorotsky 曾坚信 “ 原油决定⼀切（ As goes oil, so goes everything else ） ” ，但他现在承认，在
当前的市场语境下，逻辑已经演变为 “AI 决定⼀切（ As goes AI, so goes everything else ） ” 。

尽管短期内⾯临压⼒，但 Privorotsky 并未完全悲观。他认为现货市场的算⼒价格每天都在创出
新⾼，需求依然 “ 贪婪且永⽆⽌境 ” 。在交易策略上，他表⽰虽然⽬前信念度不⾼，但已遭遇⼤幅
降级的 “ 电⽓化 ” 相关资产、⺠⽤航空航天资产以及部分运输板块，⽬前已经具备了可观的安全边
际，值得保持谨慎乐观。

的那个交易，是做多  AI、做空其他⼀切。它已经有点变成了、很⼤的⼀笔交易。现在随着我们
